July 20, 2021

HONORABLE MAYOR AND MEMBERS OF THE CITY COUNCIL:

On behalf of City Administration and staff, I respectfully present to you the FY 2022 Proposed
Budget for the City of Alice, Texas. I offer to you this transmittal letter, which is intended as a
summary and overview of the information provided in greater detail within the FY 2022
Proposed Budget Document.
While this budget document includes hundreds of pages full of facts and numbers, it is important
to remember that the budget is really about how the City, as an organization, serves our
residents, businesses, and visitors.
According to the budgeting standards established by the Government Finance Officers
Association of the United States and Canada (GFOA), a municipal budget document should be a
policy document, an operations guide, a financial plan, and a communications medium.
This proposed budget document attempts to present and explain each fund separately. Each fund
section includes supplemental information which illustrates where the money is being allocated,
number of employees compensated from the fund, historical funding information to include
ADOPTED, REVISED, PROPOSED, and other pertinent data. Other sections of this document,
entitled Statistical Section/Appendices provide additional information, including past results.
This information is provided to illustrate to the public the process used by Council to allocate
scarce resources to meet the changing and challenging needs in our community of
approximately, 19,285. (U.S. Census Bureau, Population Division).
Summary of FY 2022 Proposed Budget
1.) This proposed budget presents a balanced budget for the following funds:
• General Fund – surplus
• Solid Waste Fund – surplus
• Utility Fund – surplus
• Airport Fund – balanced
• Golf Course Fund – balanced
• Multi-Use Complex – balanced

A

It is important to note that the Airport, Golf Course, and Multi-Use Complex Fund are balanced
due to inter-fund transfers from the General Fund.
2.) Several years back the citizens of Alice voted in Collective Bargaining for both the Police
and the Fire Departments. This means we are obligated to negotiate with both
Associations. Generally these terms are every three years. This year was one of those
years and during several months of negotiations the City and the Associations were able
to come into agreement on a few main goals. Both associations agreed to work to
becoming fully staffed departments in order to curb overtime and restructuring new pay
scales. The Police Associations agreed to include a larger increase in entry level pay of
3% to attract more applicants and a lower increase of 1.5% for current officers. With
other changes in the contract a large percentage of the pay increase are offset with cuts
and changes in other areas. The Fire Association agreed to waive certain rights to pay
allocation increases as well as built in accrued increases in exchange for a 1% base pay
increase.
3.) This budget is based on the assumption the City will have the same tax rate as FY 2021.
The sum total of the proposed budget is more than just a compilation of “bottom line” numbers.
It is the foundation upon which we annually establish our commitment to the public. It is the
single most important document produced by the City, and as you read through the budget, I am
sure you will realize the great efforts that have been made by prior City Councils and current
members returning this City to Financial Stability.
The City of Alice was a vibrant and growing community. Dependent on the oil and gas industry,
the City suffered severe economic loss with the decline and halt to oil and natural gas drilling in
January of 2015. By May 2015, major layoffs occurred throughout the City, resulting in a loss of
tax revenue, lower property values, and a higher rate of unemployment. In fiscal year ending
2016, the City collected $5,612,396 less sales tax than 2015. At that point in time, City
leadership was faced with very hard decisions - decisions that were at times unpopular amongst
some of their constituents. However, these decisions had to be made. At this point in time, three
years ago, the City of Alice revised FY 2016 budged showed a deficit of $5,397,912; the
proposed budget presented to the City Council for FY 2017 included a $4,026,885 deficit. City
Leadership was faced with the challenge of reducing the deficits while at the same time
maintaining the services needed for the protection and welfare of our citizens.
The decision by prior Councils to raise the tax rates was not something that was done without
strict consideration to reduce expenses. Since measures and initiatives to adjust to the sudden
economic decline were not timely implemented, Administration and the City Council had to find
ways to immediately minimize the shock. In May of 2016, the salaries of Non Civil Service
Personnel were impacted through a 10% reduction in pay. In the FY 2022 budget, there was not
a salary adjustment for non-civil service employees however it is important to note that year to
date non-civil service employees have received a salary adjustment of 8.5%.

B

The state of the economy and changing conditions outlined above ensure that funding is limited
for the 2021-2022 Budget. Accordingly, emphasis is placed on funding City priorities.
The first priority is to make sure that the Budget includes funding to a level sufficient to pay
outstanding debt obligations, and to maintain current levels of service to citizens.
Another essential Budget priority involves retaining and replenishing sufficient fund balance
reserves. Adequate fund balances are necessary for cash flow requirements and unforeseen
contingencies. The Budget is developed with attention to the concept of long-term sustainability.
That is, even though fund balances might be drawn upon to fund a specific one-time project or to
fill a temporary need, regular operating expenses should be covered by dependable and
consistent sources of revenue. Likewise, any one-time or temporary revenues should be used for
one-time projects, and not depended upon for on-going funding of basic City services.
To achieve these priorities requires that we take a strategic look at our organization, establishing
revenues to meet budget obligations, reducing expenditures, and streamlining processes.
During the upcoming budget work sessions, we hope the Mayor and City Council will recognize
the significant efforts made by all City personnel to curtail spending and to seek cost reductions
without affecting City services.
HOW THE CITY BUDGET IS ORGANIZED:
The City of Alice operates on an October 1st to September 30th fiscal year. The Budget is
organized into separate and distinct “Funds,” each of which is described in detail in the Budget
Document.
Budgeting is on a modified accrual basis for all Funds. Under the modified accrual basis of
accounting, revenues are recognized only when they become measurable and available to finance
expenditures of the fiscal period. Expenditures are recognized when the fund liability is
incurred.
SECTION I - GENERAL FUND
The first Fund presented in the Budget Document is the GENERAL FUND. The General Fund
accounts for all revenues and expenditures of the City which are not accounted for in other
funds. It is the largest of the City’s funds thereby receiving a greater variety and number of taxes
and other general revenues than any other fund. It is the City’s primary tax supported Fund, used
to account for all funds not required by standard budgeting practice to be budgeted separately.
The General Fund’s resources also finance a wider range of activities than any other fund. Major
functions financed by the General Fund include administrative, finance, library, garage, criminal
investigation, uniform patrol, fire suppression, park maintenance and street maintenance.

C

General Fund Balance History
The impact of the Eagle Ford Shale caused the City’s General Fund Balance to dramatically
fluctuate over the last decade. The actual FYE 2021 estimated fund balance is $7,657,120. This
reflects approximately 7.75 months of operating expenses.
Primary Revenues Impacted
The Primary Revenues that impacted the General Fund were: Ad Valorem Taxes and Sales Tax.
Ad Valorem Taxes for Maintenance and Operation (M&O) are generally a critical component to
a City’s General Fund. For the City of Alice, the Certified Values has been as follows:

The certified 2022 Taxable Assessed Valuation for the City of Alice is $819,593,399. This is a
2.6% increase from the prior year.
Impact of Taxable Values on the Effective Tax Rate
As outlined in State Law, an important component for Truth in Taxation requires cities and other
entities to calculate the Effective Tax Rate based upon on the New Taxable Assessed Valuation.
The effective tax rate creates the foundation to generate the same amount of tax levy as the prior
year. The proposed budget presented to you includes revenues equating to the same dollar
amount of property tax revenue as adopted in FY 2020. Since the certified 2020 Taxable
Assessed Valuation for the City of Alice has decreased, in order to raise the same amount of ad
valorem tax revenues as the 2019-2020 year, the City must adopt its effective tax rate.

Primary Revenues Impacted – Sales Tax Revenue
Sales Taxes are also generally a critical component to the City’s General Fund. For the first
eight (8) months of FY 2021, sales tax collections for the City of Alice increased by 1.68% for
the same time period as the prior year.

D

For the City of Alice, sales taxes have generated the following revenues:
•
•
•

2019 - $6,407,064, reflecting 48% of the General Fund Revenues
2020 – $5,324,020, reflecting 41% of the General Fund Revenues
2021 - $5,400,000, reflecting 46% of the General Fund Revenues

The General Fund is the main accounting source for tracking financial transactions for the City.
This fund’s 2021 projected excess of revenue over expenditures is $10,109.

E

ENTERPRISE FUNDS
The City operates five ENTERPRISE FUNDS. These funds are designed to be self-supporting,
meaning the funds derive their revenues from fees for services rather than taxes. These
ENTERPRISE FUNDS include: Solid Waste, Utilities, Airport, Golf Course, and the Multi-Use
Complex. Currently the Airport, Golf Course, and the Multi-Use Complex are operating with
deficit spending. A transfer of funds from the General Fund to the Golf Course, Airport and
Multi-Use Complex is required to maintain operations.
SECTION II – SOLID WASTE FUND
In FY 2015, Council approved the separation of Solid Waste Operations to its own Enterprise
Fund. This was done in an effort to make this operation self-sustaining. The function of the
Solid Waste Fund is to provide for the collection of garbage, including brush, and its
transportation for disposal at the City's Landfill. This fund is also responsible for remaining in
compliance with all applicable statutes and regulations. The FY 2021 budget show an excess of
revenues over expenditures of $86,834. The proposed budget for FY 2022 projects revenues of
$3,119,075 and expenditures of $3,030,988, showing an excess of revenues over expenditures of
$88,087. The projected ending fund balance at the close of FY 2022 is estimated at $3,076,962,
equating to approximately 12.18 months of operating expenses.
Beginning with FY 2021, the Solid Waste Fund transferred $500,000 to the General Fund as an
Interfund Transfer. An Interfund Transfer is a legally authorized transfer between funds. This
transfer is considered an Overhead allocation, which is common for many municipalities. These
transfers are to allocate certain costs generally incurred in the General Fund to the respective
enterprise funds. Examples of these overhead allocations include City Hall Operations and
allocation of Management Staff Time. These transfers are generally calculated on a true cost
basis. In an effort to minimize the General Fund deficit, the proposed budget for FY 2022
includes an estimated interfund transfer of $610,000.

F

G

SECTION III – UTILITY FUND
The function of the Utility System Fund is to provide for a source of water supply, its
purification, and distribution to customers. The fund also provides for the sanitary collections,
treatment and disposal of wastewater on a user fee basis. The projections for the budget for FY
2021 show a surplus of revenues over expenditures of $166,487. The proposed budget for FY
2022 projects revenues of $8,690,015 and expenditures of $8,458,271. The projected ending
fund balance at the close of FY 2022 is $4,354,014. Similar to the Solid Waste Fund, the City of
Alice has historically transferred amounts from the Utility System to the General Fund. For FY
2021, the interfund transfer from the Utility Fund to the General Fund was $1,385,000. In the
proposed budget for FY 2022 an estimated inter-fund transfer from the Utility Fund to the
General Fund is at $1,565,000.

H

SECTION IV – AIRPORT FUND
This Fund’s function is to account for the operations of the Alice International Airport. The City
of Alice and Jim Wells County jointly operate the airport and the two parties each hold an
undivided interest in the assets and liabilities related to the airport. The City and County are
each responsible for reporting separately their one-half share of individual assets, liabilities,
revenues and expenses of the airport operations. For FY 2021, $47,025 from the General Fund
was budgeted as an inter-fund transfer to the Airport Fund. In 2022, a transfer in the amount of
$78,093.20 from the General Fund to the Airport Fund is required to maintain operations.
Finance Staff is working to develop processes and procedures to make this fund self-sustaining.
The proposed budget for FY 2022 includes an estimated inter-fund transfer of $63,845 to the
General Fund for overhead allocations.

I

SECTION V – GOLF COURSE FUND
The Golf Course fund accounts for the operations of the Alice Municipal Golf Course. For FY
2021, $168,559 from the General Fund was budgeted as an inter-fund transfer to the Golf Course
Fund. A transfer from the General Fund in the amount of $124,908.05 is included in the
proposed budget for FY 2022. A transfer from the General Fund to the Golf Course Fund is
required to maintain operations. Similar to the Airport Fund, Finance Staff is evaluating existing
sources of revenue to achieve self-sustainability. The proposed budget for FY 2022 includes an
estimated inter-fund transfer of $70,075 to the General Fund for overhead allocations.

J

SECTION VI – MULTI-USE COMPLEX FUND
The Multi-Use Complex includes a Natatorium and Outdoor Leisure Pool. This Fund’s function
is to account for the operations of the Alice Multi-Use Complex. The facility opened its doors to
the public in late June 2016. In December of 2016, the City Council suspended aquatic
operations pending further evaluation, and a cost analysis of the facility operating year round vs
the facility operating seasonally. On June 22, 2017, aquatic operations resumed. For FY 2021,
$198,894 from the General Fund was budgeted as an inter-fund transfer to the MUC Fund. The
proposed FY 2022 budget includes a transfer in the amount of $197,894 from the General Fund
has been included. For FY 2021, the City entered into an agreement with YMCA to run the
facility, we expect to continue this agreement in FY 2022.

K

SECTION VII – DEBT SERVICE FUND
The City of Alice Budget contains one DEBT SERVICE FUND, which is used to record the
annual payments on outstanding debt, including, but not limited to, General Obligation Bonds
and Certificates of Obligation. The money needed to make these payments comes from the debt
levy and interfund transfers. Similar to the impact the Taxable Assessed Valuation had on
Maintenance and Operations (M&O), the City’s Debt Service or Interest and Sinking (“I&S”)
Tax Rate was also affected. Two new Texas Water Development Board Bonds and PPFCO
were added in FY 2020. In understanding the Council’s goal of trying to minimize the impact
of the City’s total tax rate, City Staff proposes to utilize $209,119 of the I&S Fund Balance. It is
important to emphasis that by law, monies in the I&S Fund can only be utilized to pay debt
service.
SECTION VIII – CAPITAL IMPROVEMENT PROGRAM
Protecting, maintaining, and replacing the City’s infrastructure such as: Water and Wastewater
Lines, Facilities, Equipment, Municipal Fleet, Technology and Programs are one of the most
important aspects of any municipality. Beginning in December 2016, City Staff began the
process of identifying areas to include in the Capital Improvement Program (CIP). The criteria
used by staff in the development of the CIP included:
•
•
•
•
•
•

Aging Infrastructure
Existing facility or system needs rehabilitation/replacement/upgrade
Aging Equipment and Fleet
Safety Concerns
Maintenance efforts are no longer satisfactory
Maintenance costs exceed projected replacement costs

SECTION IX – HOTEL OCCUPANCY TAX FUND
The Hotel Occupancy Tax Fund is a SPECIAL REVENUE FUND. Special Revenue Funds are
used to account for the process of specific revenue sources that are legally restricted to
expenditures for specific purposes. Hotel Occupancy Tax is a tax on the rental of rooms that is
required by the City of Alice.

In summary when consolidating all operating funds, the FY 2022 Proposed Budget is a balanced
budget dependent upon certain assumptions and the adoption of the no new revenue tax rate.
This budget was submitted to the Mayor and City Council and made available to the public on
July 20, 2021. We look forward to obtaining the feedback and input from City leadership and
our citizens.

L

M

SECTION I
GENERAL FUND

1

SECTION I
GENERAL FUND
The General Fund accounts for all revenues and expenditures of the City which
are not accounted for in other funds. It is the largest of the City’s funds and
receives a greater variety and number of taxes and other general revenues than
any other fund. The General Fund’s resources also finance a wider range of
activities than any other fund. Major functions financed by the General Fund
include administrative, finance, library, garage, criminal investigation, uniform
patrol, fire suppression, park maintenance and street maintenance.

GENERAL FUND
10200 - CITY COUNCIL

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THE CITY OF ALICE OPERATES UNDER A HOME RULE CHARTER ADOPTED IN 1949, THAT AUTHORIZED
THE COUNCIL - MANAGER FORM OF MUNICIPAL GOVERNMENT. THE CITY COUNCIL IS COMPOSED OF A
MAYOR AND FOUR COUNCILMEMBERS WHO ARE ELECTED BY PLACE FOR NON-STAGGERED TWO-YEAR
TERMS. THE COUNCIL IS CHARGED WITH THE RESPONSIBILITIES OF ENACTING LEGISLATION, ADOPTING
THE BUDGET, SETTING THE TAX RATE, SETTING POLICY GUIDELINES AND APPOINTING THE CITY
MANAGER.

GENERAL FUND
10700 - ADMINISTRATION - COMMAND CENTER

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THIS DIVISION HAS BEEN SPECIFICALLY CREATED TO FACILITATE THE OPERATIONS OF THE COMMAND
CENTER FACTILITY THAT IS BEING CONVEYED BY THE UNITED STATES ARMY THROUGH A FEDERAL
PROGRAM AS PART OF THE BRAC PROCESS (BASE REALIGNMENT AND CONSOLIDATION). THE TWO MAIN
PURPOSES OF THE COMMAND CENTER ARE TO PROVIDE A JOINT LAW ENFORCEMENT AND FIRE
TRAINING CENTER AND TO SERVE AS AN ALTERNATE EMERGENCY OPERATIONS CENTER.

THE ADMINISTRATION - COMMAND CENTER HAS NO FTE'S.

GENERAL FUND
33200 - FIRE DEPARTMENT

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THE FIRE DEPARTMENT IS COMPOSED OF TWO DIVISIONS. THE SUPPRESSION DIVISION IS
RESPONSIBLE FOR EXTINGUISHING FIRES, MAINTAINING EQUIPMENT AND KEEPING PERSONNEL
PROPERLY TRAINED, AND PROTECTING THE LIVES AND PROPERTY OF THE CITIZENS OF ALICE AND
THE SURROUNDING AREA.
THE FIRE PREVENTION DIVISION IS RESPONSIBLE FOR INVESTIGATING THE CAUSE AND ORIGIN OF
EACH FIRE, PUBLIC EDUCATION, AND INSPECTION. THE FIRE PREVENTION DIVISION WILL ALSO
OVERSEE THE INSPECTIONS AND PUBLIC HEALTH - CODES DEPARTMENTS.

GENERAL FUND
43500 - MUNICIPAL COURT

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THE PRIMARY FUNCTION OF THE MUNICIPAL COURT IS TO MAINTAIN RECORDS OF
CITATIONS ISSUED FOR VIOLATION OF STATE LAW AND CITY ORDINANCES THAT FALL
UNDER ITS JURISDICTION. IT IS RESPONSIBLE FOR THE DOCUMENTATION OF ALL CASES
FILED IN THE COURT FROM THE TIME A CITATION OR COMPLAINT IS RECEIVED THROUGH
THE COURT PROCESS AND FINAL DISPOSITION. THIS REQUIRES THAT THE COURT CLERK
MAINTAIN ACCURATE RECORDS OF CASES FILED IN THE MUNICIPAL COURT, DOCKET ALL
CASES ARISING BEFORE THE COURT, NOTE DISPOSITION OF THOSE CASES, COLLECT
FINES, PREPARE REPORTS FOR THE STATE COMPTROLLERS OFFICE AND MAINTAIN
RECORDS OF COURT CASES.
THE MUNICIPAL COURT JUDGE IS UNDER CONTRACT WITH THE CITY.

43616 - POLICE - CADET PROGRAM

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THIS DIVISION IS FOCUSED ON HIRING QUALIFIED INDIVIDUALS TO ATTEND THE DEL MAR

SECTION II
SOLID WASTE FUND

73

SECTION II
SOLID WASTE OPERATIONS FUND
This Fund’s function is to provide for the collection and disposal of trash, garbage
and brush and its transportation to the City's Landfill. This fund is also
responsible for remaining in compliance with all applicable statutes and
regulations.

SECTION IV
AIRPORT FUND

114

SECTION IV
AIRPORT FUND
This Fund’s function is to account for the operations of the City-County Municipal
Airport. The City of Alice and Jim Wells County jointly operate the airport and the
two parties each hold an undivided interest in the assets and liabilities related to
the airport. The City and County are each responsible for reporting separately
their one-half share of individual assets, liabilities, revenues and expenses of the
airport operations.

AIRPORT FUND
70200 - AIRPORT OPERATIONS

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THE PAVED, LIGHTED RUNWAYS AND HANGER FACILITIES OF THE CITY-COUNTY AIRPORT
PROVIDE AN ESSENTIAL CENTER OF COMMUNICATIONS FOR PRIVATE AVIATION, CHARTER
FLIGHTS AND THE LARGER NUMBER OF BUSINESS FLIGHTS TO AND FROM AREAS, AS WELL
AS A CONTROL POINT FOR THE FEDERAL AVIATION AGENCY OPERATIONS FOR SOUTH
TEXAS. THE AIRPORT HAS AN AUTOMATED SURFACE OBSERVING SYSTEM, WHICH
PROVIDES UPDATED WEATHER INFORMATION EVERY 60 SECONDS 24 HOURS PER DAY.

MULTI-USE COMPLEX FUND
88000 - NATATORIUM

ADOPTED

2022

FUNCTION:
THE NATATORIUM SWIM CENTER PROVIDES A SAFE, SANITARY PLACE FOR ALL CITIZENS OF
ALICE AND THE PUBLIC TO ENJOY AQUATIC ACTIVITIES YEAR ROUND. THE INDOOR POOL IS
USED FOR A VARIETY OF ACTIVITIES AND FUNCTIONS INCLUDING COMPETITION SWIMMING AND
DIVING EVENTS, RECREATIONAL SWIMMING, PHYSICAL THERAPY, WATER AEROBICS, AND
YOUTH AND ADULT SWIMMING CLASSES. THE SEASONAL OUTDOOR LEISURE POOL PROVIDES A
SAFE, SANITARY PLACE WHERE ALL CITIZENS AND FAMILIES OF ALICE AND THE PUBLIC MAY
SPEND THEIR LEISURE HOURS RELAXING ON A HOT SUMMER DAY IN VARIOUS AGE
APPROPRIATE WATER ZONES OR WITH INTERACTIVE WATER FEATURES, WATER SLIDES AND
SHADE FEATURES.

THE MULTI-USE COMPLEX HAS NO FTE'S.

APPENDICES

232

APPENDIX A:
CLASSIFICATIONS BY OBJECT CLASS
Personal Services. Salaries and Wages. Amounts paid to both permanent and temporary government employees,
including personnel substituting for those in permanent positions. This category includes gross salary for personal
services rendered while on the payroll of the government.
51000

Salaries. Full-time, part-time and prorated portions of the costs for work performed by employees of the
government.

51100

Overtime. Amounts paid to employees of the government in either temporary or permanent positions
for work performed in addition to the normal work period for which the employee is compensated.

51110

Temporary Wages/Extra Help. Full-time and prorated portions of the costs for work performed by
employees of the government who are hired on a temporary or substitute basis.

51120

Seasonal Wages. Full-time, part-time and prorated portions of the costs for work performed by
employees of the government who are hired on a seasonal basis.

Personal Services – Employee Benefits. Amounts paid by the government on behalf of employees; these amounts
are not included in the gross salary, but are in addition to that amount. Such payments are fringe benefit
payments and, although not paid directly to employees, is part of the cost of personal services.
51200

Salary Reserve

51300

FICA – Social Security. Employer’s share of social security paid by the government

51400

Employee Health & Life Insurance. Employer’s share of any insurance plan

51500

TMRS - Retirement. Employer’s share of any state or local employee retirement system paid by the
government, including the amount paid for employees assigned to federal programs.

51600

Longevity

51700

Workers’ Compensation. Amounts paid by the government to provide worker’s compensation insurance
for its employees. These charges may be distributed to functions in accordance with the budget.

51750

Unemployment Benefits. Amounts paid by the government to provide unemployment compensation for
its employees. These charges may be distributed to functions in accordance with the budget.

51800

Car Allowance.

51900

Other Employee Benefits. Employee benefits other than those classified above. Government may
establish subcodes locally for various accrued amounts, such as unused compensated absences and car
allowances. Such amounts may be distributed to the functions according to the employee’s assignment.

Supplies. Articles and commodities which are consumed or materially altered when used. Following are the
principal types of supplies:
52000

Office Supplies Pens, pencils, staples, calculator tape, invisible tape, etc.

233

52010

Paper Copier paper, color paper, cardstock, specialty paper for printing

52200

Wearing Apparel Clothing allowance, safety & protective gear.

52300

Fuel Fuel purchased from a service station or in bulk; oil and lubricants.

52410

Vehicle Repair & Maintenance Supplies

52420

Equipment Repair & Maintenance Supplies

52430

Facility Repair & Maintenance Supplies

52440

Garage Chargeout Expenses

52450

Utility Chargeout Expenses

52465

Cushion Sand/Gravel/Caliche

52470

Garage Chargeout Oil & Grease

52480

Emergency Operation Supplies Items purchased during an emergency/natural disaster, etc.

52481

Employee Award Supplies Items for retirees during employee appreciation awards ceremony

52482

Street Repair and Maintenance Supplies Caliche, hot/cold mix, gravel, etc.

52483

Engineering Supplies

52485

Water/Wastewater Repair & Maintenance Supplies

52487

Golf Green Supplies

52490

Evidence Supplies

52500

Chemicals Expenditures for chemicals used for water and wastewater treatment, aquatic chemicals.
Disinfectants.

52520

Hazmat & Foam Supplies

52550

Ammunition

52560

In-House Training Supplies Expenditures for training supplies for sessions performed by the police
department training officer.

52600

Inventory Items purchased for use in operations and charged to departments as used.

52650

Inventory/Discarded Unusable inventory items.

52800

Printing & Publishing Expenditures for the printing of standard forms, photo printing, business cards,
name plates, magnets for city council members.

52810

Printer/Copier Supplies Printer cartridges, toner, waste containers, etc.

234

52820

Postage & Freight Expenditures for overnight mailings/shipping, mailing of lab samples, etc., postage
stamps, metered postage, freight on packages.

52830

First Aid/Medical Supplies

52840

Laboratory/Testing Supplies

52850

Janitorial Supplies Expenditures for cleaning products; i.e. toilet paper, paper towels, cleaning solutions,
etc.

52860

Catering/Meal Expenses Food and drinks for human consumption, supplies needed for serving food at
meetings, workshops, employee recognition banquets, etc.

52870

Weed/Pest Control Supplies

52880

Park & Athletic Field Supplies Sand, gravel, field marking paint, etc.

52890

Animal Care Animal food, veterinary visits, kenneling, training equipment, litter, food/water dishes, etc.

52910

Books Expenditures for books, textbooks and periodicals available for general use, including reference
books.

52920

Subscriptions

52940

Rollout/Dumpster Supplies

52950

Small Tools and Minor Equipment Small equipment expenditures of less than $5,000.00.

52960

Minor Information Technology Equipment Expenditures of less than $5,000.00. Computer software and
peripherals can be charged here if less than $5,000.00; digital cameras.

52990

Bank Charges

Purchased Property Services. Services purchased to operate, repair, maintain and rent property owned or used by
the government. These services are performed by persons other than government employees. Although a
product may or may not result from the transaction, the primary reason for the purchase is the service provided.
54010

Electricity

54020

Natural Gas

54030

Propane

54100

Other Water Purchases Expenditures for water services from a private or public utility company.

54200

Vehicle Repair & Maintenance Services Expenditures for repair and maintenance services for vehicles not
provided directly by government personnel. Services mechanics, machinists, etc., that include charges for
parts or materials along with charges of labor, vehicle repairs contracted to vendors (tire repair, car
washes, state inspection sticker, etc.)

235

54230

Equipment Repair & Maintenance Services Expenditures for repair and maintenance services for
equipment not provided directly by government personnel. Services mechanics, machinists, etc., that
include charges for parts or materials along with charges of labor, equipment repairs contracted to
vendors (radio repair, weed eater repair, SCBA fit tests, etc.)

54250

Facility Repair & Maintenance Services Expenditures for repair and maintenance services for buildings
not provided directly by government personnel. Services plumbers, electricians, HVAC technicians, etc.,
that include charges for parts or materials along with charges of labor, building repairs contracted to
vendors. These expenditures include contracts and agreements covering the upkeep of buildings. Costs
for renovating and remodeling are not included here but are classified under object code 5490 (elevator
maintenance, filter service, cleaning services, mat/towel service, etc.)

54263

Library Services

54264

Sanitation Services

54265

Water/Wastewater Services

54280

Street Repair & Maintenance Services

54300

Equipment Hire & Rentals Expenditures for equipment rentals i.e. folding machine, heavy equipment,
pumps, etc.

54310

Copier Lease Expenditures for monthly lease payment

54320

Copier Maintenance/Overages Copier overage charges

54330

Telephone Leases Expenditures for monthly lease payment

54400

Billing Statement Processing Services

54480

Emergency Management Services

54500

Incident Repairs

54610

Tire Disposal Services

54620

Document Management Services Disposal service of confidential documents

54640

Document Archiving Services

54870

Weed & Pest Control Services

54880

Park & Athletic Field Services

54900

Construction Services Includes amounts for constructing, renovating and remodeling paid to contractors
less than $5,000.00.

236

Other Purchases Services. Amounts paid for services rendered by organizations or personnel not on the payroll of
the government (separate from professional and technical services or property services). Although a product may
or may not result from the transaction, the primary reason for the purchase is the service provided.
55010

Telephone Service

55020

Cell Phone Service

55030

Internet

55040

Television Service Cable, Satellite television service.

55200 Insurance Other than employee benefits. Expenditures for all types of insurance coverage including
property, liability and fidelity. Insurance for group health is not charge here, but is recorded under object code
51400.
55300

Community Relations Halo-Flight donation, event sponsorships, community-event expenses, etc.

55400

Travel Expenses Expenditures for transportation, meals, hotel and other expenses associated with staff
travel for the government. Payments for per diem in lieu of reimbursements for subsistence (room and
board) are also charged here.

55410

Tuition Expenses

55700

Advertising Expenditures for announcements in professional publications, newspapers or broadcasts
over radio and television. These expenditures include advertising for such purposes as personnel
recruitment, legal ads, ordinances, new and used equipment and sale of property.

55701

Advertising – Planning & Zoning

Purchased Professional and Technical Services. Services that by their nature can be performed only by persons of
firms with specialized skills and knowledge. Although a product may or may not result from the transaction, the
primary reason for the purchase is the service provided. Included are the services of architects, engineers,
auditors, dentists, physicians, lawyers and consultants. A separate account should be established for each type of
service provided to the government.
56100

Official/Administrative Services in support of the government’s various policy-making and managerial
activities. These services include management consulting activities directed toward general governance
or business and financial management of the government, tax-accessing and collecting services.

56101

Notary Bonds

56110

Memberships & Dues Memberships and dues in professional organizations.

56120

Plat Recording Fees

56130

Election Fees/Services

56140

Jail Contract Expense

56150

Plat Filing Expense

237

5620
Professional Services Services provided by a skilled, certified and/or specialized person or entity. These
services include library and media support and contracted instructions services (speakers).
56210

Licenses & Certifications

56290

State Fees TCEQ Fees

56300

Other Professional Professional services, other than educational that supporting the operation of the
government. These professionals include systems analysts and planners, promotional advertising, scale
certification.

56301

Background Check Expenses Pre-employment screening, DPS searches and name searches

56310

Legal – City Attorney

56320

Legal – Municipal Court Judge

56330

Legal – City Prosecutor

56340

Engineering Services

56360

Audit Services

56361

Actuarial Services

56380

Medical Evaluation & Services. Includes employee physicals and other medical services, therapists.

56390

Technical Service

56500

GPS Monitoring Service for GPS monitoring and rental of equipment for vehicles/equipment

56800

YMCA Offset Loss

238

Property. Expenditures for acquiring fixed assets, including land or existing building, improvements of grounds
initial equipment, additional equipment and replacement of equipment.
65000

Information Technology Externally acquired software and computer peripherals over $5,000.

67000

Land Expenditures for the purchase of land.

68000

Buildings and Improvements Expenditures for acquiring existing buildings. These expenditures include
the principal amount of capital lease payments resulting in the acquisition of buildings, except payments
to building authorities or similar agencies. Expenditures for the contracted construction of buildings, for
major permanent structural alterations and for the initial or additional installation of hearing and
ventilation systems, fire protection systems and other service systems in existing buildings are recorded
under object code 5490. Buildings constructed and alterations performed by the government’s own staff
are charge to object code 54250 if less than $5,000.00 or 68000 if greater than $5,000.00.

68300

Paving System Improvements Expenditures, including seal coating, for roadways with paving only.

68400

Paving and Drainage System Improvements Expenditures, including seal coating, for roadways with
curbs, curbs and gutters, and/or storm drainage.

69000

Machinery and Equipment Expenditures for the initial, additional and replacement items of equipment
such as machinery, furniture and fixtures and vehicles.
Furniture and Fixtures Expenditures for furniture and fixtures including furniture and building
fixtures.
Machinery Expenditures for equipment usually composed of a complex combination of parts
(excluding vehicles). Examples are lathes, drill presses and printing presses.
Vehicles Expenditures for equipment used to transport persons or objects. Examples include
automobiles, trucks and heavy equipment.

239

APPENDIX B:
GLOSSARY
ACTUAL BUDGET – Actual amount of revenue or expenditure for the last completed year.
AD VALOREM TAX – General property taxes levied on an assessed valuation of real and or personal
property.
APPROPRIATION – A legal authorization granted by a legislature body to make expenditures and to incur
obligations for specific purposes.
APPROPRIATION ORDINANCE - An ordinance by means of which appropriations are given legal effect
ASSESSED VALUATION – A valuation set upon real estate or other property by a government as a basis
for levying taxes.
AWA - Alice Water Authority.
BEGINNING BALANCE – The net working capital of a fund on the first day of the fiscal year.
BUDGET – A plan of financial operation embodying an estimate of proposed expenditures for a given
period and the proposed means of financing them.
CAPITAL OUTLAY – Expenditures that result in the acquisition of or addition to fixed assets.
CAPITAL BUDGET – Plans of proposed capital outlays and the proposed means of financing them.
CHARTER – The authoritative document that provides for the incorporation of our City form of
government and other general previsions as approved by a majority of the qualified voters.
CITY COUNCIL – A mayor and four councilmen who shall be elected by the majority of qualified voters
in an election held and governed by the General Laws of the State of Texas.
DEBT SERVICE – The payment of general long-term debt principal and interest.
DEBT PER CAPITA – The share of net bonded indebtedness of each citizen in the City of Alice.
ENDING BALANCE – The net working capital of a fund on the last day of the fiscal year.
EFFECTIVE TAX RATE – The tax rate when applied to the current assessed valuation will generate the
same tax levy as the previous year.
FUND – A fiscal and accounting entity with a self-balancing set of accounts which are segregated for the
purpose of carrying on specific activities or attaining certain objectives in accordance with special
regulations, restrictions, or limitations.
FUND TYPES – The eight generic fund types and three broad fund categories.
GOVERNMENTAL FUNDS
General Fund – The General Fund is the general operating fund of the City. It is used to account for all
financial resources except those required to be accounted for in another fund.
Special Revenue Funds – Special Revenue Funds are used to account for the proceeds of specific revenue
sources (other that special assessments, expendable trusts, or major capital projects) that are legally
restricted to expenditures for specific purposes.

240

Debt Service Funds – Debt Service Funds are used to account for accumulation of resources for, and the
payment of, general long-term debt principal, interest, and related costs.
Capital Projects Fund – Capital Projects Funds are used to account for financial resources to be used for the
acquisition or construction of major capital facilities (other than those financed by proprietary funds,
Special Assessment Funds, and Trust Funds).
Special Assessment Funds – Special Assessment Funds are used to account for the financing of public
improvements or services deemed to benefit the properties against which special assessments are levied.
PROPRIETARY FUNDS
Enterprise Funds – Enterprise Funds are used to account for operations (a ) that are financed and operated
in a manner similar to private business enterprises-where the intent of the governing body is that the costs
(expenses, including depreciation) of providing goods or services to the general public on a continuing basis
be financed or recovered primarily through user charge; or ( b ) where the governing body has decided that
periodic determination of revenues earned, expenses incurred, and/or net income is appropriate for capital
maintenance, public policy, management control, accountability, or other purposes.
Internal Service Funds – Internal Service Funds are used to account for the financing of goods or services
provided by one department or agency to other departments to agencies of the City or to other
governments, on cost-reimbursement basis.
FIDUCIARY FUNDS
Trust and Agency Funds – Trust and Agency Funds are used to account for assets held by the City in a
trustee capacity or as an agent for individuals, private organizations, other governments, and/or other funds.
These include Expendable Trust, Nonexpendable Trust, Pension Trust, and Agency Fund. Nonexpendable
Trust and Pension Trust Funds are accounted for in essentially the same manner as proprietary funds since
capital maintenance is critical. Expendable Trust Funds are accounted for in essentially the same manner as
governmental funds. Agency Funds are custodial in nature (Assets equal liabilities) and do not involve
measurements of results of operations.
FRANCHISE – A special privileged granted by a government permitting the continuing use of public
property, such as city streets, usually involving the elements of monopoly and regulation.
FUND BALANCE – The fund equity of governmental funds and trust funds.
FISCAL YEAR – A 12 month period to which the annual operating budget applies.
GENERAL OBLIGATION BOND – Bonds for the payment of which the full faith and credit of the issuing
government are pledged.
INTERGOVERNMENTAL REVENUES – Revenues from other governments in the form of grants,
entitlements, shared revenues, or payment in lieu of taxes.
LETTER OF TRANSMITTAL – Introduces reader to the budget document.
NET WORKING CAPITAL – A fund’s current assets less its current liabilities.
OPERATING BUDGET – Plans of current expenditures and the proposed means of financing them.
PROPOSED BUDGET – Estimated amount of revenue and expenditures to be realized for the proposed
budget year.

241

REVISED BUDGET – Estimated amount of revenue and expenditures to be realized at current year end;
the revised budget estimates are calculated using a least six months of actual revenues and expenditures
data.
RESERVE – ( 1) an account used to earmark a portion of fund balance to indicate that it is not appropriate
for expenditure ( 2 ) an account used to earmark a portion of fund equity as legally segregated for a specific
future use.
SALES TAX – A tax imposed upon the sale of consumption of goods or services generally with a few or
limited exemptions.
SPECIAL ASSESMENT – A compulsory levy made against certain properties to defray part of the cost of a
specific improvement or service deemed to primarily benefit those properties.
RETAINED EARNINGS – An equity account reflecting the accumulated earning of an enterprise or
internal service fund.
TAX LEVY – The total amount of taxes or special assessments imposed by a government.
TAX ORDINANCE – An ordinance by means of which taxes are levied.
INTERFUND TRANSFER – Legally authorized transfers from a fund receiving revenues to the fund
through which the resources are to be expended.
USER CHARGE – Payment of a fee for direct receipt of a public service by the party benefiting from the
service.

242

Industrial Waste (Ord. 1341-1286 Sec. 22-157)
(a) Charge determination; routine sampling and testing:
If the volume or character of the waste to be accepted by the city is such that it will not overload the sewage collection, pumping, handling, treating, or
disposal facilities of the city, then prior to such acceptance, the city and the industrial concern shall enter into an agreement whereby the industrial
concern shall make payment of an industrial waste charge to be determined as set forth below:

The Industrial charge shall be calculated by the following:

The determination of flow, B.O.D., and suspended materials shall be made by the city laboratory and if the firm or industry concerned shall send samples to an independent
laboratory, then the cost of the sample shall be paid solely by the firm or industry. The time of selection of the sample shall be at the sole discretion of the city, but at least on an
annual basis to adjust the industrial charge to reflect the current costs of wastewater treatment. The applicable surcharge determined by such tests shall be retroactive for two
(2) billing periods and shall continue for six (6) billing periods unless subsequent tests determine that the surcharge should be further increased.

(b) Surcharge for exceeding limits; routine re-testing:
Before establishing a surcharge, the owner must be notified in writing that the waste discharge from his property exceeds the maximum limits as established in this division. If
after three (3) consecutive monthly inspections, the quality has not been brought within the maximum allowed, the customer must enter into a surcharge agreement or be
disconnected from the city's sanitary sewer system. Thereafter the flow will be routinely inspected each six (6) months and if found above or below the maximum allowed, the
surcharge will be adjusted accordingly.

Computations of each surcharge, as applicable, shall be based on the following:
S = V x 8.34 x .075 x (B.O.D. - 200); and
S = V x 8.34 x .075 x (S.S. - 200).

Private Fire Protection Water Rate (Ord.of 6/23/75 Sec. 22-58)
Private fire protection water rates for nonmetered service by the city shall be the net annual charge for water service made available
when such charges are paid in advance or before January 31 of each year such service is to be rendered.
Otherwise such charges shall be subject to a penalty of increased charges in the amount of ten (10) percent of the net charges if
payment is made on or before March 1 of the current year. Should payment of charges not be made on or before March 1 of the
current year, water service shall be discontinued. If paid after March 1 a turn-on fee of three dollars ($3.00) shall be made.

Building Permit Fees (Continued)

B104 Penalties
Where work for which a permit is required by this code is started or proceeded
prior to obtaining said permit, the fees herein specified shall be doubled, but the
payment of such double fee shall not relieve any persons from fully complying
